It really seemed to start around the time they rolled out the new standardized curriculum at my school. Since then, I've just gradually pulled away from my usual activities.\n\nDoctor: During this 14 months, have you also had periods of feeling depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I've felt deeply depressed or down in that specific way, to be honest. It feels more like I'm just completely empty and lacking any of my usual drive. I'm not sure if that counts as being depressed, but the motivation is just entirely gone.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you had any periods of feeling clearly depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt depressed, sad, or hopeless in that specific way. It’s more that I just feel completely empty and entirely lack any of my usual drive or motivation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 years when you felt low energy or lost interest most days, even if you were not feeling sad or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been going on for two years. This severe loss of energy and interest only started about 14 months ago when the new curriculum was introduced. Before that, I was actually very active and highly engaged in my teaching and community theater.\n\nDoctor: Did the loss of interest and motivation begin within about 3 months of the curriculum change, and has it mainly been tied to that stressor? Also, has it caused clear problems in your work, relationships, or daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Yes, it started right around the time the curriculum rolled out and it was definitely tied to that stress, though it has become much deeper than just burnout. It has caused major problems at work, where my teaching evaluations dropped and I was nearly placed on a performance improvement plan. I've also had to step back from coaching and the theater, which has really strained my relationships with my colleagues, students, and friends.\n\nDoctor: Have you also had symptoms like intrusive memories of the curriculum change, nightmares, feeling constantly on edge, or avoiding reminders of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience nightmares, intrusive memories, or feel constantly on edge or jumpy like that. Even though the curriculum changes have been a major source of stress and worry for me, I don't have those kinds of trauma-like symptoms.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels like my spark has just gone out and I feel completely flat inside. I simply can't find the drive or care enough to start anything, even though I know my life and work are slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had any other depression symptoms such as poor sleep, changes in appetite or weight, low energy, trouble concentrating, or feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my sleep, appetite, or feelings of guilt. It's mostly just this profound lack of drive and energy to do things that I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Did the symptoms begin within 3 months of the curriculum change, and do you feel noticeably better when you are away from that stressor or after it has been reduced?"

Yes, it definitely started within a couple of months of the curriculum rollout. However, I don't feel any better when I'm away from school; even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch with absolutely no energy or motivation to work on my lesson plans or theater scripts.

Has it been present most days for at least two years, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It hasn't been as long as two years, but it's been building for a little over a year now, about 14 months. It really seemed to start around the time they rolled out the new standardized curriculum at my school. Since then, I've just gradually pulled away from my usual activities.\n\nDoctor: During this 14 months, have you also had periods of feeling depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I've felt deeply depressed or down in that specific way, to be honest. It feels more like I'm just completely empty and lacking any of my usual drive. I'm not sure if that counts as being depressed, but the motivation is just entirely gone.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you had any periods of feeling clearly depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt depressed, sad, or hopeless in that specific way. It’s more that I just feel completely empty and entirely lack any of my usual drive or motivation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 years when you felt low energy or lost interest most days, even if you were not feeling sad or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been going on for two years. This severe loss of energy and interest only started about 14 months ago when the new curriculum was introduced. Before that, I was actually very active and highly engaged in my teaching and community theater.\n\nDoctor: Did the loss of interest and motivation begin within about 3 months of the curriculum change, and has it mainly been tied to that stressor? Also, has it caused clear problems in your work, relationships, or daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Yes, it started right around the time the curriculum rolled out and it was definitely tied to that stress, though it has become much deeper than just burnout. It has caused major problems at work, where my teaching evaluations dropped and I was nearly placed on a performance improvement plan. I've also had to step back from coaching and the theater, which has really strained my relationships with my colleagues, students, and friends.\n\nDoctor: Have you also had symptoms like intrusive memories of the curriculum change, nightmares, feeling constantly on edge, or avoiding reminders of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience nightmares, intrusive memories, or feel constantly on edge or jumpy like that. Even though the curriculum changes have been a major source of stress and worry for me, I don't have those kinds of trauma-like symptoms.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels like my spark has just gone out and I feel completely flat inside. I simply can't find the drive or care enough to start anything, even though I know my life and work are slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had any other depression symptoms such as poor sleep, changes in appetite or weight, low energy, trouble concentrating, or feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my sleep, appetite, or feelings of guilt. It's mostly just this profound lack of drive and energy to do things that I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Did the symptoms begin within 3 months of the curriculum change, and do you feel noticeably better when you are away from that stressor or after it has been reduced?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely started within a couple of months of the curriculum rollout. However, I don't feel any better when I'm away from school; even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch with absolutely no energy or motivation to work on my lesson plans or theater scripts.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts of wanting to end my life or hurting myself. Even though I feel completely unmotivated, empty, and overwhelmed by how much is slipping away, those kinds of thoughts haven't crossed my mind."
  }

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I feel like I’ve lost the drive and interest in everything I used to care about, even my students and the arts stuff I loved. Has it been present most days for at least two years, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It hasn't been as long as two years, but it's been building for a little over a year now, about 14 months. It really seemed to start around the time they rolled out the new standardized curriculum at my school. Since then, I've just gradually pulled away from my usual activities.\n\nDoctor: During this 14 months, have you also had periods of feeling depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I've felt deeply depressed or down in that specific way, to be honest. Even though the curriculum changes have been a major source of stress and worry for me, I don't have those kinds of trauma-like symptoms.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels like my spark has just gone out and I feel completely flat inside. I simply can't find the drive or care enough to start anything, even though I know my life and work are slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had any other depression symptoms such as poor sleep, changes in appetite or weight, low energy, trouble concentrating, or feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my sleep, appetite, or feelings of guilt. It's mostly just this profound lack of drive and energy to do things that I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Did the symptoms begin within 3 months of the curriculum change, and do you feel noticeably better when you are away from that stressor or after it has been reduced?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely started within a couple of months of the curriculum rollout. However, I don't feel any better when I'm away from school; even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch with absolutely no energy or motivation to work on my lesson plans or theater scripts.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts of wanting to end my life or hurting myself. Even though I feel completely unmotivated, empty, and overwhelmed by how much is slipping away, those kinds of thoughts haven't crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a similar episode of loss of interest or low mood before this, or is this the first time it has happened? Also, has the stressor become less intense over time, and if so, did your symptoms improve at all?\n\nPatient: This is the first time I have ever felt this way, as I've never experienced anything like this before. The stress at school hasn't really gotten any better, and my symptoms certainly haven't improved either.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been severe enough to make you miss work, avoid responsibilities, or struggle to function in your daily life most days? Also, do you feel this has been present nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely impacted my daily life; over this past school year, my teaching evaluations have dropped, I nearly ended up on a performance improvement plan, and I had to step back from coaching and theater because I just couldn't manage them anymore. This has definitely been happening almost daily for much longer than two weeks, as this loss of drive and pulling away has been building up for over a year now, starting around the time the new curriculum was introduced.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel emotionally or physically better when the school stress is reduced or when you are away from work, or do the symptoms stay the same regardless of the stressor?\n\nPatient: No, the symptoms stay the same regardless of whether I'm at school or away from it. Even when I am at home on weekends, I just sit on the couch, unable to muster up any motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. Being away from the work environment doesn't make me feel any better or bring my drive back."

Also, do you feel this has been present nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely impacted my daily life; over this past school year, my teaching evaluations have dropped, I nearly ended up on a performance improvement plan, and I had to step back from coaching and theater because I just couldn't manage them anymore. This has definitely been happening almost daily for much longer than two weeks, as this loss of drive and pulling away has been building up for over a year now, starting around the time the new curriculum was introduced.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel emotionally or physically better when the school stress is reduced or when you are away from work, or do the symptoms stay the same regardless of the stressor?\n\nPatient: No, the symptoms stay the same regardless of whether I'm at school or away from it. Even when I am at home on weekends, I just sit on the couch, unable to muster up any motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. Being away from the work environment doesn't make me feel any better or bring my drive back.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from the school stress, do you ever feel noticeably better or more like yourself, or do the loss of interest and low energy stay the same no matter where you are?\n\nPatient: No, being away from school doesn't help at all, and the lack of drive remains completely constant no matter where I am. Even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch and can't find the motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. This loss of interest is definitely present everywhere, not just when I'm at school.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or has it mainly been the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been mainly the profound loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad, depressed, or hopeless. It feels like my spark has completely gone out and I am just going through the motions of life. I just can't seem to care enough to start anything, rather than feeling deeply sad."

It’s more that I just feel completely empty and entirely lack any of my usual drive or motivation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 years when you felt low energy or lost interest most days, even if you were not feeling sad or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been going on for two years. This severe loss of energy and interest only started about 14 months ago when the new curriculum was introduced. Before that, I was actually very active and highly engaged in my teaching and community theater.\n\nDoctor: Did the loss of interest and motivation begin within about 3 months of the curriculum change, and has it mainly been tied to that stressor? Also, has it caused clear problems in your work, relationships, or daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Yes, it started right around the time the curriculum rolled out and it was definitely tied to that stress, though it has become much deeper than just burnout. It has caused major problems at work, where my teaching evaluations dropped and I was nearly placed on a performance improvement plan. I've also had to step back from coaching and the theater, which has really strained my relationships with my colleagues, students, and friends.\n\nDoctor: Have you also had symptoms like intrusive memories of the curriculum change, nightmares, feeling constantly on edge, or avoiding reminders of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience nightmares, intrusive memories, or feel constantly on edge or jumpy like that. Even though the curriculum changes have been a major source of stress and worry for me, I don't have those kinds of trauma-like symptoms.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels like my spark has just gone out and I feel completely flat inside. I simply can't find the drive or care enough to start anything, even though I know my life and work are slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had any other depression symptoms such as poor sleep, changes in appetite or weight, low energy, trouble concentrating, or feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my sleep, appetite, or feelings of guilt. It's mostly just this profound lack of drive and energy to do things that I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Did the symptoms begin within 3 months of the curriculum change, and do you feel noticeably better when you are away from that stressor or after it has been reduced?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely started within a couple of months of the curriculum rollout. However, I don't feel any better when I'm away from school; even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch with absolutely no energy or motivation to work on my lesson plans or theater scripts.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts of wanting to end my life or hurting myself. Even though I feel completely unmotivated, empty, and overwhelmed by how much is slipping away, those kinds of thoughts haven't crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a similar episode of loss of interest or low mood before this, or is this the first time it has happened? Also, has the stressor become less intense over time, and if so, did your symptoms improve at all?\n\nPatient: This is the first time I have ever felt this way, as I've never experienced anything like this before. The stress at school hasn't really gotten any better, and my symptoms certainly haven't improved either.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been severe enough to make you miss work, avoid responsibilities, or struggle to function in your daily life most days? Also, do you feel this has been present nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely impacted my daily life; over this past school year, my teaching evaluations have dropped, I nearly ended up on a performance improvement plan, and I had to step back from coaching and theater because I just couldn't manage them anymore. This has definitely been happening almost daily for much longer than two weeks, as this loss of drive and pulling away has been building up for over a year now, starting around the time the new curriculum was introduced.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel emotionally or physically better when the school stress is reduced or when you are away from work, or do the symptoms stay the same regardless of the stressor?\n\nPatient: No, the symptoms stay the same regardless of whether I'm at school or away from it. Even when I am at home on weekends, I just sit on the couch, unable to muster up any motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. Being away from the work environment doesn't make me feel any better or bring my drive back.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from the school stress, do you ever feel noticeably better or more like yourself, or do the loss of interest and low energy stay the same no matter where you are?\n\nPatient: No, being away from school doesn't help at all, and the lack of drive remains completely constant no matter where I am. Even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch and can't find the motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. This loss of interest is definitely present everywhere, not just when I'm at school.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or has it mainly been the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been mainly the profound loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad, depressed, or hopeless. It feels like my spark has completely gone out and I am just going through the motions of life. I just can't seem to care enough to start anything, rather than feeling deeply sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been feeling depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling down?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels more like a complete emptiness or a loss of my 'spark' where I just can't bring myself to care or start any tasks, rather than feeling down."

However, the duration is now over a year and the symptoms are not limited to the stressor context or relieved by being away from it, which makes a depressive episode still possible despite the lack of classic low mood. So the differential remains the same, with adjustment-related disorder still favored over a depressive episode, but depressive episode not fully excluded.","is_final":false}

Being away from the work environment doesn't make me feel any better or bring my drive back.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from the school stress, do you ever feel noticeably better or more like yourself, or do the loss of interest and low energy stay the same no matter where you are?\n\nPatient: No, being away from school doesn't help at all, and the lack of drive remains completely constant no matter where I am. Even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch and can't find the motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. This loss of interest is definitely present everywhere, not just when I'm at school.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or has it mainly been the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been mainly the profound loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad, depressed, or hopeless. It feels like my spark has completely gone out and I am just going through the motions of life. I just can't seem to care enough to start anything, rather than feeling deeply sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been feeling depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling down?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels more like a complete emptiness or a loss of my 'spark' where I just can't bring myself to care or start any tasks, rather than feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you also had a depressed, sad, or hopeless mood most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling down?"

It just feels like the spark I used to have has completely gone out, and I'm struggling to even get through the day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this loss of interest and low drive? Has it been present most days for at least two years, or did it start more recently?\n\nPatient: It hasn't been as long as two years, but it's been building for a little over a year now, about 14 months. It really seemed to start around the time they rolled out the new standardized curriculum at my school. Since then, I've just gradually pulled away from my usual activities.\n\nDoctor: During this 14 months, have you also had periods of feeling depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I've felt deeply depressed or down in that specific way, to be honest. It feels more like I'm just completely empty and lacking any of my usual drive. I'm not sure if that counts as being depressed, but the motivation is just entirely gone.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you had any periods of feeling clearly depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt depressed, sad, or hopeless in that specific way. It’s more that I just feel completely empty and entirely lack any of my usual drive or motivation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 years when you felt low energy or lost interest most days, even if you were not feeling sad or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been going on for two years. This severe loss of energy and interest only started about 14 months ago when the new curriculum was introduced. Before that, I was actually very active and highly engaged in my teaching and community theater.\n\nDoctor: Did the loss of interest and motivation begin within about 3 months of the curriculum change, and has it mainly been tied to that stressor? Also, has it caused clear problems in your work, relationships, or daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Yes, it started right around the time the curriculum rolled out and it was definitely tied to that stress, though it has become much deeper than just burnout. It has caused major problems at work, where my teaching evaluations dropped and I was nearly placed on a performance improvement plan. I've also had to step back from coaching and the theater, which has really strained my relationships with my colleagues, students, and friends.\n\nDoctor: Have you also had symptoms like intrusive memories of the curriculum change, nightmares, feeling constantly on edge, or avoiding reminders of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience nightmares, intrusive memories, or feel constantly on edge or jumpy like that. Even though the curriculum changes have been a major source of stress and worry for me, I don't have those kinds of trauma-like symptoms.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels like my spark has just gone out and I feel completely flat inside. I simply can't find the drive or care enough to start anything, even though I know my life and work are slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had any other depression symptoms such as poor sleep, changes in appetite or weight, low energy, trouble concentrating, or feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my sleep, appetite, or feelings of guilt. It's mostly just this profound lack of drive and energy to do things that I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Did the symptoms begin within 3 months of the curriculum change, and do you feel noticeably better when you are away from that stressor or after it has been reduced?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely started within a couple of months of the curriculum rollout. However, I don't feel any better when I'm away from school; even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch with absolutely no energy or motivation to work on my lesson plans or theater scripts.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life is not worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts of wanting to end my life or hurting myself. Even though I feel completely unmotivated, empty, and overwhelmed by how much is slipping away, those kinds of thoughts haven't crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a similar episode of loss of interest or low mood before this, or is this the first time it has happened? Also, has the stressor become less intense over time, and if so, did your symptoms improve at all?\n\nPatient: This is the first time I have ever felt this way, as I've never experienced anything like this before. The stress at school hasn't really gotten any better, and my symptoms certainly haven't improved either.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been severe enough to make you miss work, avoid responsibilities, or struggle to function in your daily life most days? Also, do you feel this has been present nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has severely impacted my daily life; over this past school year, my teaching evaluations have dropped, I nearly ended up on a performance improvement plan, and I had to step back from coaching and theater because I just couldn't manage them anymore. This has definitely been happening almost daily for much longer than two weeks, as this loss of drive and pulling away has been building up for over a year now, starting around the time the new curriculum was introduced.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel emotionally or physically better when the school stress is reduced or when you are away from work, or do the symptoms stay the same regardless of the stressor?\n\nPatient: No, the symptoms stay the same regardless of whether I'm at school or away from it. Even when I am at home on weekends, I just sit on the couch, unable to muster up any motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. Being away from the work environment doesn't make me feel any better or bring my drive back.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from the school stress, do you ever feel noticeably better or more like yourself, or do the loss of interest and low energy stay the same no matter where you are?\n\nPatient: No, being away from school doesn't help at all, and the lack of drive remains completely constant no matter where I am. Even when I'm at home, I just sit on the couch and can't find the motivation to work on my theater scripts or lesson plans. This loss of interest is definitely present everywhere, not just when I'm at school.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or has it mainly been the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It has definitely been mainly the profound loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad, depressed, or hopeless. It feels like my spark has completely gone out and I am just going through the motions of life. I just can't seem to care enough to start anything, rather than feeling deeply sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been feeling depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling down?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation, rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels more like a complete emptiness or a loss of my 'spark' where I just can't bring myself to care or start any tasks, rather than feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you also had a depressed, sad, or hopeless mood most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling down?\n\nPatient: Yes, as I mentioned, it is definitely the loss of interest and drive rather than feeling actively sad or depressed. It just feels like my spark has completely gone out, and I'm simply going through the motions. I don't feel hopeless, just entirely empty of motivation.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms start within about 3 months of the school curriculum change, and have they stayed closely tied to that stressor rather than occurring on their own?"

I'm not sure if that counts as being depressed, but the motivation is just entirely gone.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you had any periods of feeling clearly depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt depressed, sad, or hopeless in that specific way. It’s more that I just feel completely empty and entirely lack any of my usual drive or motivation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 years when you felt low energy or lost interest most days, even if you were not feeling sad or hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely hasn't been going on for two years. This severe loss of energy and interest only started about 14 months ago when the new curriculum was introduced. It has caused major problems at work, where my teaching evaluations dropped and I was nearly placed on a performance improvement plan. I've also had to step back from coaching and the theater, which has really strained my relationships with my colleagues, students, and friends.\n\nDoctor: Have you also had symptoms like intrusive memories of the curriculum change, nightmares, feeling constantly on edge, or avoiding reminders of it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't experience nightmares, intrusive memories, or feel constantly on edge or jumpy like that. Even though the curriculum changes have been a major source of stress and worry for me, I don't have those kinds of trauma-like symptoms.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt depressed, sad, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, or is it mainly the loss of interest and motivation without feeling sad?\n\nPatient: It is definitely mainly the loss of interest and motivation rather than feeling actively sad or hopeless. It feels like my spark has just gone out and I feel completely flat inside. I simply can't find the drive or care enough to start anything, even though I know my life and work are slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had any other depression symptoms such as poor sleep, changes in appetite or weight, low energy, trouble concentrating, or feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any issues with my sleep, appetite, or feelings of guilt. It's mostly just this profound lack of drive and energy to do things that I've been struggling with.\n\nDoctor: Did the symptoms begin within 3 months of the curriculum change, and do you feel noticeably better when you are away from that stressor or after it has been reduced?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely started within a couple of months of the curriculum rollout. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
